--- Commit Message ---
LU-19921 ptlrpc: add SEC_CTX_INIT ctx RPC handler

This patch implements server-side termination of the GSSIAM security
context initialization (SEC_CTX_INIT) handshake and connection
context installation, establishing the foundation for cryptographic
token verification in subsequent patches.

Specifically:
- In lustre/ptlrpc/gss/sec_gssiam.c, gssiam_accept() delegates
  context initialization and connect RPCs to gss_svc_accept(),
  which decomposes the security context inside the RPC.
- gssiam_handle_init() handles the SEC_CTX_INIT RPC, creates an
  ephemeral server security context (gss_rsc), generates a 64-bit
  context handle, and replies to the client.
- In lustre/ptlrpc/sec.c and lustre/ptlrpc/service.c, generalize the
  unwrapping check to test req->rq_reqmsg != req->rq_reqbuf instead
  of checking specific security policies. This ensures that embedded
  messages are cleanly unpacked and their swab masks are reset
  across all policies and hybrid framing models, while direct
  null-framed requests preserve their initial unpack state.
- Export lustre_msg_set_cksum() in lustre/ptlrpc/pack_generic.c
  for message integrity operations.

Signed-off-by: Di Wang <ddiwang@google.com>

Some concerns, see inline comments. There is also no test coverage for the new handler; is a sanity-sec case planned once the flavor is registered?

(3 comments)

AI code review results below, please doublecheck

Mostly good; only style-level comments inline. The handshake flow traces cleanly end to end: gssiam_accept() -> gss_svc_accept() -> gss_svc_handle_init() -> gssiam_handle_init() mints the rsc entry and 8-byte handle, and the follow-on GSS-framed connect resolves it via gss_svc_upcall_get_ctx() -> gss_svc_searchbyctx(). The reply layout also checks out: with src_init set before packing, gss_svc_alloc_rs() gives buflens[0] = PTLRPC_GSS_HEADER_SIZE (44), exactly sizeof(struct gss_rep_header) + 8, so the handle memcpy fits. The sec.c / service.c switch from a policy test to `rq_reqmsg != rq_reqbuf` is equivalent for null, plain and gss (gss_svc_unseal_request() sets rq_reqmsg to lustre_msg_buf(reqbuf, 0, 0), which is never rq_reqbuf), so no existing flavor changes behavior.

Mostly good, minor comments inline. The sec.c/service.c generalisation to `req->rq_reqmsg != req->rq_reqbuf` is faithful for the registered policies - null_accept_common() leaves the two equal, and plain_accept()/gss_svc_verify_request()/gss_svc_unseal_request() all point rq_reqmsg at a segment inside the wrapper, so no existing flavor changes behaviour. Everything under the gssiam flavor is still dead code (sptlrpc_gssiam_policy is never passed to sptlrpc_register_policy(), per the XXX in sptlrpc_gssiam_init()), so none of the new handler can be exercised yet; whichever patch registers the policy should bring sanity-sec coverage for the SEC_CTX_INIT handshake with it.

One cross-cutting note: gssiam_handle_init() hands back a fully usable server context before any token checking exists (the verification is deferred to a later patch), so the whole handshake currently authenticates whoever asks. That is only safe because sptlrpc_gssiam_init() still leaves the policy unregistered. Worth keeping that dependency explicit in the series so the patch that registers the gssiam policy lands after the one that verifies the token, not before.
